ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-22-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00155_d7e3996	Federal Minister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-22-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00155_d7e4012	Ladies and gentlemen! There are guidelines for the activities of the State Commissioners, or its alternate to credit institutions within the meaning of the Banking Act and to pension funds. In principle, the State Commissioners and their alternate bodies are the Financial Market Supervisory Boards. In other words, they are subject to financial market supervision and are bound by the instructions of financial market supervision. They are an essential source of information for the ongoing operational supervision of financial market supervision. You have appropriate rights and obligations. It is true that financial market supervision has a database to provide the State Commissioner with all the information on the current size of existing credit institutions' credit institutions' concessions. That is to say, it is a network where, on the one hand, the State Commissioner informs the Financial Markets Supervisory, on the other hand, after auditing the Financial Markets Supervisory, informs the State Commissioner to see if the Bank will really fix what has happened in the audit as a vulnerability. There are, of course, appropriate lectures, of course, there is a training offer for the state commissioners, so that they can actually be an effective part of the supervision.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-22-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00155_d7e4097	Ladies and gentlemen! As I have just said, the State Commissioners are not bound by the instructions of the Ministry of Finance, but by the instructions of independent non-profit financial market supervision. It does not have the right of supervision over credit institutions by the Federal Ministry of Finance within the meaning of the current legal basis of the Banking Act, but this is the case with the supervision of the financial market. It makes sense, therefore, that the State Commissioners are bound by the instructions of financial market supervision. I believe once again that the importance of the State Commissioner in the system of banking supervision is important and that on-the-spot activity makes real sense when one is involved in the Supervisory Board meetings when one has a corresponding right of question when one can question whether all the decisions taken there are in conformity with the law. If no, of course you can also object accordingly. One can see the documents, the mediums of the credit institution. I therefore believe that there are substantial opportunities available to the State Commissioner here, so that the Internal Revision chain – Supervisory Board – Auditors – State Commissioners – is something that makes absolutely sense.

With Austria's accession to the Convention on compensation for victims of violence to be decided today, an extension of the protection of victims is not only taking place in Austria. This Convention of the Council of Europe lays down the rules for Member States which are members of the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union for the payment of earnings, health, treatment and hospital expenses, funeral costs and failure of maintenance. As in the form of upper and lower limits, application et cetera is also regulated. Austria can now accede to this Convention, since third-country nationals have also been admitted to the Crime Victim Act with the novelle 2005. In Austria we go beyond the requirements of this Convention, since all third-country nationals, if the act is committed in Austria, on an Austrian ship or in an Austrian aircraft the right to compensation, and not just those nationals of the Member States of the Council of Europe. However, a restriction had to be made: that the act did not occur before the in-force step of the novelle, so only after the 30th century. ==References== As mentioned above, Austria regulates aid in the crime victim law, which provides for more extensive aid than it does in the Convention. At present, the World Cup is in all its mouths. An example to illustrate: An Austrian football fan goes to a match in a country that has joined this convention. In the event of outcries, as unfortunately happened in France years ago, this fan is seriously injured. He now has the right to compensation, whether the perpetrator is known or not. Another example is that in Austria, thanks to God not as often as elsewhere: A foreigner is beaten up because of racism. This is the Foreigners, if they legally stay in Austria – visitors as well as asylum seekers – are entitled to these assistances under the Crime Victim Act. As a psychotherapist, however, I am also proud that Austria has been providing psychotherapy for victims of violence in the crime victim law for a long time. Precisely psychological consequences due to the traumatization of violence are often longer or heavier to process than physical damage. But I should also like to address something that is especially dear to my heart on the subject of violence. It must also be said that 20 States of the Council of Europe have now ratified this scheme; Austria will, I hope, do so unanimously in a few minutes. We will then, together, undertake to pay compensation according to the situation of the case: compensation for loss of earnings, health care costs, hospital expenses, medical expenses, funeral expenses. What is also very important: maintenance costs are also affected. The condition must be that this offence has been committed on Austrian territory or that it has been committed on an Austrian aircraft or ship. It is also important to mention that the victims have their permanent stay in our Republic. Ladies and gentlemen, it is also important to mention that there is a claim for compensation even if, no matter what reason, the perpetrator cannot be persecuted or punished. These are the reasons why our group will vote in favour of this ratification. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-22-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00155_d7e11112	We now come to points 7 and 8 of the agenda on which the debate is being held under one. There was no oral reporting. First speaker is Mrs Steibl. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-22-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00155_d7e11144	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The Austrian engineering title enjoys high reputation not only in Austria but also beyond our country. In view of the large number of administrative procedures necessary for the award of this title, this law now seeks simplification. The current system is based on two conditions: at least three years of professional practice, which requires higher professional knowledge, in the field on which the maturity test has been carried out. This law relieves these conditions by requiring only more than three years of professional practice, which requires up-to-date knowledge in those areas on which mature examinations can be carried out. The criterion of professional practice is thus replaced by a pure practice. It is also important that the law adapts to the rapid change of the professional world. With this reform, we are taking an important step in this direction. The higher education schools offer a variety of specialisation options that do not always fit exactly with the term “higher knowledge”. Ladies and gentlemen! In this context, it should be noted that: We need to create work to give security. Thanks to German Chancellor Wolfgang Schüssel and the employment initiative “Company Workplace”, for example, €300 million in addition to the basic budget of the AMS has been used for this purpose. I think that in recent years we have created a large number of jobs with this government, well over 120,000 new jobs. It is also important to think about new forms of work. With “new forms of work” I mean not work on call, but in many areas the much quoted part-time work. Part-time work models are increasing, and they are not always as negatively occupied as it is repeatedly claimed. It is also not true that women are being pushed down in part-time work, because in Finland or Greece, about half of all women are involuntarily employed part-time, in Austria, between 6 and 9 percent of women aged 25 to 49. – I therefore believe that you should already think about whether a number of statements are actually being handled with care. I would also like to mention that what is going on in economic growth, Austria is far ahead, third in all European countries. Since 2003, Austria has even been above the EU's average – and this, although the new Eastern European Member States are booming. The average economic growth is 1.7 percent. Finally: Social is what creates work. And: Those who create work make sure they're safe. This change of law represents an important step in the future.

To the Federal Law on the status name ‘Ingenieur’: The aim of this law is debureaucratisation. What are the concrete objectives of this federal law? – Simplification of the credibility of engineering activities, a rapid and unbureaucratic award, a debureaucratisation of administrative procedures – and this with an acceleration of procedures. I would like to make this very clear today: In order for the Austrian Federal Army to be able to fulfil in the future these achievements which we have presented in the Federal Force Reform Commission, so that we can carry out domestic tasks that are still necessary – the protection of the population and their livelihoods, security police assistant missions, disaster missions and, of course, airspace surveillance, which our soldiers have carried out again in the last few days, but also all foreign activities – so that we can have a stable environment and live safely in Austria, we need three pillars: We need the cadre staff, we need the militia, and we need the groundmen. If one of these pillars is cut, we will no longer be able to perform these achievements as it has been in the past! Therefore, I am glad that we now have a military officer legally installed. The militia is necessary for filling the battalions and brigades on the one hand. Secondly, the militia is necessary for the national battalions that we are currently setting up in the various federal states, and thirdly as an expert pool, so that this know-how, which these militia soldiers have acquired in their professional life, is ultimately also available to the Austrian federal army. So it's about having a legally installed military officer. I think this is a very progressive way, but it is also the way to make a clear commitment to the military not only in Sunday's speeches, but also that the decisions are taken accordingly. ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-22-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00155_d7e12789	Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! We are today adopting a novel to the Military Power Act. As has already been said, the independence of the legal guardians is at the centre. In principle, however, the Federal Constitutional Act provides for the binding of instructions on organs, provided that no constitutional exception is made. In the field of national defence, this can be found in Article 80(1). 3, according to which the Federal Minister for the Defence of the State has the power of command over the Federal Army and all its divisions. That means: The legal protection officer would also be bound against him, although, as we have already heard, no instructions have been given by our Federal Minister Platter in this regard. But there was a certain curious situation now. In particular, the opposition parties were repeatedly demanded that the legal protection officer be set free. On the other hand, we need the approval of the large opposition party, otherwise the two-thirds majority will not be granted. The provisions on the legal protection officer under the Security Police Act now came a breakthrough. Now the situation is such that the security officer is re-trained with the federal army of this position. The legal protection officer with his two alternates, all three of whom have the same rights and obligations, is independent and free of discretion, they are subject to the confidentiality of office, they have access to all relevant documents and they can enter all the spaces in which these documents are located. On this occasion, I would also like to thank the SPÖ and Toni Gaál especially for the fact that we are today bringing about the necessary constitutional majority. The exclusion statements also appear important to me that one assumes that the Federal Minister of State Defense provides the necessary personnel to the legal protection officer and that, in the exercise of his duties, this staff is subject only to the instructions of the legal protection officer and that the Federal Minister for State Defense gives no instructions to the assigned staff. Since it has also been said that it should be an institution of the National Council, I would like to say: I think it is very important that the Legal Protection Officer should be present annually until 31. The report shall be submitted on March and shall be made available to the Standing Subcommittee of the National Council if required. – This gives the necessary feedback to the National Council. As I said, I am pleased with the constitutional agreement to the amendment to the Military Power Act.

[[Abg. Mandak: Here comes the police when a child doesn't have lunch?]] Therefore, this measure has to be put in place. [[Abg. Mandak: Why are the police getting it? There can be a social worker!]] One more time. This child has neither been arrested nor taken into custody, but it has been picked up to care for it. [[New calls to the Greens]] The mother was then brought together with the child and cared for as a relief. This answer has already been given in the letter. Question 14: The amount of support allocated in 2004 amounted to € 501 755. In 2005 it was €529 994. For 2006, €530 000 is estimated. These funds are covered in the budget. Question 15: After the work on the shift care contracts in 2006 has been practically completed, the results to date are evaluated and adaptations are being developed together with all the partners. The internal preparatory work is complete and the corresponding resources are also available in the current budget. On the question 16: According to § 3 Family compensation law, persons who are not Austrian citizens are entitled to family allowances only if they legally reside in Austria in accordance with Sections 8 and 9 of the Constitutional and Residence Act. However, the NAG is not applicable to asylum seekers. Therefore, asylum seekers are not entitled to family aid. But that has been the case so far. Their security of life is guaranteed by public authorities in the form of the basic supply system. Only after recognition as a refugee under the Geneva Convention of Refugees have Austrians been treated in this regard. On the question 17: Of course, the marriage with an Austrian alone cannot replace all the other legally required conditions. The Foreign Police Authority has therefore already been instructed – from the specific case to the point of view – that before the appointment of a foreign police measure, the jurisdiction of the decision of the branch authority is to be expected. Derogations exist only in the event of a genuine prohibition of residence or in the event of convictions of imprisonment. The case cited here was withdrawn after the marriage of the asylum application, and after that a residence permit was requested. In this phase, the woman was illegally resident in Austria. That too is a legal situation! The procedures have now continued to run, and we have given this direction precisely because of this case. On the question 18: Such an initiative is not proposed. This is because it would contradict the objective and purpose of the principle of maintaining orderly immigration. Initial applications should therefore be made to professional representatives abroad. It is added, however, that in certain cases, for example for spouses of Austrians, an domestic application is also provided for after legal entry and lawful residence. On question 19: To this end, it is reported that the Federal Ministry of the Interior does not record its own records. Citizenship awards are sent directly from the federal states to Statistics Austria, which in turn prepare the respective quarterly reports. Thus, for the requested period, there are no data from Statistics Austria, which were made available to the Federal Ministry of the Interior. I have tried to question the number of applications in some federal states. I was told that there were different declines: in Tyrol between 33 and 50 percent, in Styria 63 percent, in Lower Austria between 40 and 80 percent. Accurate numbers cannot be passed on yet. The new resident and resident law has reduced the number of initial authorisations for members of “advanced third-country nationals” in Austria by 76.2 percent compared to the same period of the previous year. The total number of newly issued residence permits also decreased by 76.1 percent compared to the previous year. The number of open procedures also requested: The BAA currently speaks of 6 745. We can see that more procedures are now being completed for the first time than applications are being made. This means that within three months, up to four months – if anything does not happen – we have completely reworked the backlog and that the ongoing requests can be met. The UBAS has an increase – a significant increase! – experience: The UBAS had 84 employees and was increased by 82. So there too, the procedures will be able to be handled much faster. I hope to have answered the urgent question.

I say a clear yes to those who need asylum because they have to leave their country for political or religious reasons. But I also say a clear no to those who come to our country for economic reasons. With the 2005 Foreign Rights Package, we have created a consistent and effective way to speed up proceedings, we have created tougher measures against tow and effective measures in criminal asylum seekers. I myself am – I have already mentioned it – mayor of the municipality Pfaffstätten; 250 meters from our border is the first recording center in Traiskirchen. I think that we in the district of Baden bear the main burden of asylum policy. However, we can also prove that this asylum policy has developed very positively in recent years, positively in the sense and for the benefit of the population. Mr Pendl is also sitting there; he will speak for me. He is also an in-house citizen. Together, we have really solved problems on this important issue, away from the party-political dimension. At this point I would also like to thank Mrs Innenminister Prokop, who, at the Pfaffstätten Safety Summit, also with Mr Pendl, with Mr Leiss, has tried to develop solutions. The result can be seen. We have strengthened the subjective sense of security in Traiskirchen, through more police presence and additional controls, and we have created the right framework conditions. – Thank you in this context also to the police in Traiskirchen, but also in the district of Baden. Ladies and gentlemen! Asylum and integration policy means exercising state responsibility for those concerned, but above all also for the Austrians. This Federal Government with Federal Chancellor Wolfgang Schüssel and Federal Minister Lies Prokop shoulder this responsibility and is the guest of a successful policy.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-22-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00155_d7e17869	Madam President. Mrs. Minister! High house! It's about health. On the facts very briefly: In 2005, we spent €1.8 billion on medicines. The rate of increase in the cost of medicinal products from 1998 to 1999 was double-digit. Due to structural measures and price reduction measures, these growth rates have been commuted to 3 to 4 percent in the last year, although they have been forecast at 7 to 9 percent. It has not only been decided on price damping measures, as they were set out ten, 15 years ago in the light of simplicity, but it has also been changed structurally: the establishment of the Committee on Medicinal Products Evaluation, the transfer of medical authorizations from the social insurance chief and control services to the prescribed doctor, the reduction of medicinal products authorisation procedures by the implementation of a special documentation and subsequent control. Price reduction measures such as a reduction in the trading margin, sales-related special discounts and pricing rules are also included in this package. For us, health policy means ensuring quality, including with regard to the supply of medicinal products. In particular, the authorisation of medicinal products in the European area is strictly handled, particularly in Austria, where a number of controls have to be carried out until it is finally authorised. Another important point is the distribution of medicinal products. A total of 2 232 jobs in Austria sell the products so that the medicines are accessible to everyone. However, in the implementation of the Medicines Act, it was always noted that there have been problems here for several legislative periods. On the basis of this, an authority was set up to deal with PharMed Austria, which is intended to deal with this problem. Unfortunately, nothing has been said about this exceptionally good health policy, which promises not only quality but also forward-looking, by Mr Kogler at all. He merely complained about the Court of Auditors and discussed the future of the Court of Auditors. Collaborative herbs have come with the report by Professor Rebhahn, although the footer clearly reads: This version is not intended for publication. Editments are still required for a publication. – You work there with resources and documents, which are not finished at all, and use them for your argumentation. That's how you want to make health policy? They obviously don't care about it. [[Congratulations on the Freeman – BZÖ.]] In conclusion: I have the presumption that you are not at all concerned with the matter, but probably about party policy. I am not quite sure, but it is close to the presumption, and I would ask you to take the matter seriously, because health is a matter which is really serious to take, but which you are not doing at all at present. They always say how bad everything is and that the Austrian health system has no quality. I think you yourself know that it is not, and I would ask you to address the issue a little more seriously in the future. We are really trying to find a good solution for the patients in Austria, always working forward-looking, and I think there really is no reason to think about this health policy only any bad thoughts.

I know it hurts that this Federal Government has managed to dampen drug costs – not to spend less, because that was not the goal, because we need constantly more money for health care due to the medical development, medical progress and our demographic development. We know that. But it's about that the costs don't get us out of the row. I can say that since 1990, the cost of medicinal products alone has increased by threefold, i.e. by 150 percent, and the figures speak volumes: In 1990 it was still €859 million. By 1995, they had risen by 460 million to 1.319 billion. In the next five years, between 1995 and 2000, they increased by 550 million, under the responsibility of social democrats. Minister for Health and Social Affairs. By 2005, the increase had already been a little dampened and thank God again was less than 500 million. With this drug package, which has made everyone very consciously required – namely the pharmaceutical industry, the pharmacies, the house pharmacies, the doctors in their writing practice, but also the patients in the information that generics are full medicines – we have managed to dampen the projected cost increases from 7 to 9 percent to the 3 to 4 percent we aim for. In December 2003 the House decided to enter into force in 2004: In 2004, the increase was not 7 to 9, but 3.5 percent, even 2.18 percent in 2005. That is why it is no wonder, Mr Herb, that they have risen again this year. This is relatively normal after they have fallen slightly last year. And we will take great care that they will not continue to rise. We have also done everything we can to prevent abuses in this area, that the writing practice of a doctor through natural discounts is not affected by up to 100 percent – at least that was claimed. I believe that we have found a good system to actually monitor these costs on a continuous basis, including the new authorisation procedure, including the ABS system, which is possible thanks to the health card and where the authorisation duration is currently on average between 5 and 10 minutes. I think that in these last three years, we have taken decisive, important steps in the field of health, both in terms of modernising the technology sector with the health card and in terms of cost reduction, although we all know that health costs will continue to increase in the future, on the basis of these two factors: demographic development, medical progress.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-22-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00155_d7e19350	The next item is the speech by Mr Dipl.-Ing. Hütl. Desirable time: 3 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-22-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00155_d7e19371	Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen! Mrs Moser, you should first look at health before doing such activities. I would now also like to refer to the Epidemic Act. In the course of this amendment, the law now includes the compulsory diseases currently contained in many regulations. There is already a catalogue of reported diseases, such as the bird flu virus, virus-related hemorrhagic fever, a certain influenza virus, SARS and so on, all of which comes to the large catalog of infectious diseases. An amendment also provides that the reporting obligation for laboratories is explicitly included in the law. Also, obsolete diseases are adapted to the state of science. What about the reporting obligation? – Any disease, any death of an adjudicial disease, any suspicion of such disease, shall be reported to the district administration authority within 24 hours. This authority may then issue a number of bans on the occurrence of a disease subject to reporting, such as bans on events, buildings, restrictions on traffic, and so on. I would like to point out that such an order by the Authority is an order for treatment. In the event of refusal of ordered treatment, an administrative penalty will now be initiated. There was no such possibility before. The Epidemic Act has so far not provided for the support of the Health Authority for the enforcement of various measures by the public security service bodies. A legal basis for this is now being established. I think that the extension of the reporting obligation will make a significant improvement. These changes are also very positive to our district chief Dr. Anna-Margaretha Sturm. I think, Mrs Federal Minister, that is another step in our successful health policy.

The present novel to the Epidemic Act incorporates the disease subject to notification, which is currently contained in a regulation on communicable diseases. This will for the first time lead to the Agency for Health and Food Safety, which will also have the opportunity to identify food-related outbreaks and take the necessary steps. For the first time, however, we will also be able for the first time to enable the heads of state as co-ordinators of zoonoses to fulfil their obligations under the Zoonosen Act at all, both in the federal and federal states, because they can experience them very quickly through this reporting obligation. Let me say two more sentences about the successes in the salmonella-free fight. In this area, there is a marked decline in disease. The reason is not the deterioration of the reporting morale of doctors, but the efforts of primary producers for a number of years to eliminate the occurrence of salmonella in primary production. These are measures such as the certification of free stocks with the help of the quality poultry regulation Molterer, which is still being launched by – now Klubobmann, or the voluntary vaccination of laying hens in quality programmes, as well as the compliance with far-reaching hygiene measures by the animal owners. These are just a few examples of the measures that have led to this gratifying result, including measures that are important to keep poultry in our stables. The legal provisions to be adopted today will certainly help to further improve food safety, as the end of the food chain, i.e. from the experience of consumers to obtain the relevant information. I would therefore like to thank you for your support for this law.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-22-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00155_d7e21567	Formal speaker: Mrs Grander. She too speaks for 2 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-22-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00155_d7e21590	Mr President. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Ladies and gentlemen! I would now like to refer briefly to the state of the situation in the acute hospitals and then to the rehabilitation centres, which are in the case of rehabilitation. Neurorehabilitation is a dynamic process. It is already starting in the acute phase in the hospital and is primarily under the caring motto: Help for self-help. Neurorehabilitation must be seen as a process whose quality is determined not only by the sum of the individual therapeutic units but rather by the networking of all medical, nursing and therapeutic measures into a superior whole with common goals of all professional groups. The focus of the rehabilitation process is on patients who are encouraged according to their competences, resources and shortcomings. The aim of the rehabilitation process is to reduce or compensate for the disability created, so that the people concerned can regain the greatest possible independence and the best possible quality of life and meet the requirements of their family, social and professional environment depending on the personal situation. The main objective of care in neurorehabilitation is therefore to expand and restore patient self-care skills in such a way that they can compensate the self-care deficits themselves or with the help of relatives or other caregivers and meet the self-care requirement. The aim of the care in family work is above all to impart to the family those knowledge and skills about nursing measures that they are able to use the acquired skills at home to compensate for the self-care deficits of the patients. A great deal of attention must be paid to multi-professional communication. There is also a need for regular team meetings to plan and evaluate the common patient-centred goals. They are as indispensable as the knowledge of the individual professional groups. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-06-22-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00155_d7e22216	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! Dear ladies and gentlemen! Yes, there are improvements in civil service law. For example, family hospice is also meant to be possible for the care of children of life partners. There was an injustice in the area of administrative practitioners, who were excluded from the right to travel costs allowance, but this should also be corrected now. The term ‘Professor’, which has so far only applied to pragmatized teachers, is now also intended to apply to contractually employed teachers. There are also changes to the Disciplinary Commission. Here there is a changeover decision, which means administrative simplification, faster procedures, cost savings and time savings, and that is a good thing.
